===== FILE: CHATGPT_BRIEFING.md =====
# PADEL CLUB: full development briefing (for another AI to read)

**What I want from you (ChatGPT):** read this whole document, then reply with ONE self-contained prompt that I can paste into Claude Code (the AI that has been building this game in my repository). The prompt must improve the game further. Details of the format I want are in section 14. Be critical: sections 10 and 11 list what I know is weak or unverified.

The game owner speaks Spanish; the game UI is in English. The owner is not a programmer and tests by playing.

---

## 1. What the project is

PADEL CLUB is a real-time **online multiplayer padel game** (2 vs 2, also 1 vs 1) that runs in the browser.

- **Server**: Node.js 20+/24, ES modules, `ws` WebSocket library. Authoritative: the server runs the simulation at **60 Hz** and broadcasts snapshots every tick.
- **Client**: plain JavaScript modules, **Three.js 0.170** (no build step, no bundler, no framework). The same Node server serves the static client.
- **Shared code** (`shared/`): the game rules, physics, scoring and bot AI. The identical files run on the server, in the browser (solo matches run entirely in the browser, zero latency) and in the unit tests.
- **Deployment**: Dockerfile and `render.yaml` for Render (region Frankfurt). Source on GitHub (`gonzalooocanovas/PadelClub`).
- **Size**: about 10,000 lines of JavaScript, **123 automated tests** (`node --test`), no TypeScript.
- **Tooling constraints of the environment where it is built**: Windows 11, no Python, no Blender (so no GLB 3D models: all characters, rackets and venues are procedural Three.js geometry), Node only.

### Repository layout

```
shared/   constants.js physics.js game.js scoring.js ai.js roster.js career.js career2.js story.js looks.js history.js
server/   index.js (HTTP + WebSocket)  rooms.js (rooms, matches, pause)  quick.js (matchmaking)  store.js (profiles)
client/   index.html style.css js/{main,game,scene,envs,characters,audio,input,controls-ui,ui,career-ui,drills,local,net,settings,preview}.js
test/     scoring game spin singles contact career career2 drills looks input multiplayer openings walls lob serve-shots
          pause positioning quick history story
```

## 2. Coordinate system and court

Metres. `x` across the court (-5..5), `z` along it (-10..10), `y` up. The net is the plane `z = 0`.
Team 0 defends `z < 0` and attacks toward `+z`; team 1 the opposite.

- Court 10 m x 20 m. Net height 0.88 m at the centre, 0.92 m at the posts (it sags).
- Service line 6.95 m from the net. Back walls: 3 m glass + 1 m mesh (anything above 4 m is "out").
- Side walls: **glass in the back 4 m (|z| >= 6), fence/verja (mesh) through the middle**. In 1v1 the court narrows to 7 m and the side walls are glass all along.
- **Access doors** (doubles only): in each side wall, 1.2 m to 3.2 m from the net, 2 m high. Outside each wall there is a fenced strip 1.8 m wide, 18.4 m long. A ball that has bounced once may leave through a door; the receiving team can follow it outside (players may walk out only through the doors) and must return it along the strip, round the net post and in through the other door so it lands in the opponents' court.
- Four venues: Premium Indoor Club, Mediterranean Court (outdoor), Night Arena, Sunset Rooftop. Each has its own turf bounce constants, lighting, crowd and ambience.

## 3. Physics (the part I most want improved by someone who knows the sport)

`shared/physics.js`, fixed step 1/60 s. All units SI.

**Ball (`stepBall`)**
- Gravity 9.81. Drag: linear 0.06 x 0.6 plus quadratic `0.0035 * speed` per second (fast balls slow more).
- Magnus effect: backspin `sb > 0` lifts, topspin `sb < 0` dives (`MAGNUS = 0.012`); sidespin `ss` curves left/right of the travel direction. Spin decays `exp(-0.35 dt)`.
- Floor bounce: vertical restitution per court (0.83 to 0.85) reduced by backspin and slightly by impact speed; horizontal speed kept 0.84 to 0.86 (also spin dependent); backspin halves, sidespin x0.75 per bounce.
- **Two wall materials**: glass restitution 0.82 (minus 1% per m/s up to 20 m/s), tangential friction 0.97; fence/mesh restitution 0.34, friction 0.84. Wall spin: topspin climbs the glass (`vy += -sb * 0.11 * min(1, vn/12)`), backspin drops the ball; the wall strips part of the spin; sidespin kicks the ball along the glass (0.14).
- Net: the ball hitting the net band stops (0.22 rebound); clipping the tape within 4.5 cm may pop it over ("cord") with lost pace.
- Doors/strip: described above; ball inside the strip bounces off its fences.
- Events emitted: `floor`, `wall` (axis, material), `net`, `cord`, `door`, `out`.

**Shot solver (`solveLaunch`, `simulateShot`, `planShot` in `game.js`)**
For every shot the game picks a landing point, a target flight time T and a spin, solves the launch velocity ballistically, then **simulates the real physics** (drag, spin, walls) and iteratively corrects the launch so the ball really lands there, while forcing net clearance for well-struck balls. A poor contact does not get that help.

**Racket contact (`contact()` in `game.js`)**
- Contact is deferred until the ball is within 1.2 m of the player (the "sweet spot") or the swing window is closing.
- Quality `q` (0.3..1) comes from: distance to the sweet spot (0.95 m), ball height, whether the ball is in front, running speed, running away from the ball, then a forgiving remap.
- **Fatigue**: below 45 stamina, `q` drops up to 0.25.
- **Pressure**: reaching the ball at full stretch (including a last-gasp lunge), a ball hit with great pace (>14 m/s) and being on the run reduce `q` (up to about 0.5 in total). So a good volley or a placed ball actually wins the exchange.
- Face angle from swing timing: an open face (early) gives higher, slower balls with backspin; a closed face (late) is flatter with topspin.
- Edge hits lose pace and scatter. Labels: perfect / clean / early / late / off-centre / stretched / poor.
- Bot mishits ("slip"): 60% dive into the net/floor in front, 40% go long.

**Shots**: serve, forehand, backhand, volley, lob (globo), smash (remate), bandeja, vibora, chiquita, drop shot (dejada), block, slice. Each has its own speed, depth range and spin.
- **Lob quality depends on contact**: a clean lob climbs high and lands deep (hard to smash); a poor one is low and short (easy to smash).
- **"Por 4"** (just added): a clean smash (`q >= 0.8`) from near the net, ball above 2.2 m, aimed short (depth <= 4.8 m) comes down steeply (flight time floor 0.13 s) with heavy topspin and bounces out of the court over the fence: a winner. Credited as "¡Por 4!". Bots use it with probability tied to their smash skill.
- **Serve**: the ball bounces in the server's hand (period 1.1 s). Quality depends on the ball height 0.05 s after the press (best at waist height 0.62 m, narrow window). Holding the key charges power for 0.9 s (a tap = normal; full charge = +22% pace, less accurate, **tends to fly long** past the service line). Fault rules, double fault, let-less (a net cord on the serve is a fault).
- The server stays **planted on the line** until the serve is hit; the others are frozen. After a fault everybody returns to their starting place.

**Player movement (`stepPlayer`, shared with the client for prediction)**: run 6 m/s, sprint 8 m/s, acceleration 30, deceleration 38, braking before reversing. Planted while swinging (30% speed). Stamina 100: sprint costs 24/s, **the rally itself drains 1.5/s even standing still** and only recovers between points (13/s); below 45 stamina the player is up to 18% slower. Applies to humans and bots.

## 4. Rules and scoring (`shared/scoring.js`, `game.js`)

Padel scoring: points 0-15-30-40, optional golden point, games to 6 (or configurable), sets, tiebreak, best of 1/3. Serve rotates among all four players (each team alternates its two servers). Server alternates sides. Service box diagonal. Ball must bounce once in the box. Wall before bounce = point lost for the hitter. Ball bounced and then out over the fence = point to the hitter. Second bounce = point to the hitter. Doors rules in section 2.

## 5. Multiplayer (`server/`)

- Rooms with 5-letter codes; host, ready check, team seats, AI fill, public/private, public room list, chat, quick chat, emotes, reconnection (a bot covers a dropped player; the seat is reclaimed on reconnect).
- **Quick match** (`server/quick.js`): queue; four players are matched into a doubles game; after 10 s anyone still waiting plays with bots filling the seats. The home screen shows how many players are online and the queue size.
- **Authoritative server + client prediction**: the client predicts its own player with the same `stepPlayer`, reconciles from snapshots (60 Hz), and interpolates others (0.07 s online, 0.03 s local).
- **Online pause by agreement**: a player requests a pause, every other human must accept (10 s), then the game freezes for at most 30 s or until every human presses Resume; two pauses per player; a disconnected player does not block it.
- Rate limiting, input sanitising, input queue cap, server-side validation of all settings; the client cannot submit results.
- Guest profiles (token in the browser, stored server side): XP, level, match stats.

## 6. Bot AI (`shared/ai.js`) and how it was analysed

Bots produce exactly the same inputs a human client would send (so they obey every rule). Four levels (Rookie, Club, Pro, Legend) with parameters: speed, reaction time, aim noise, reach, flub chance, timing error, aggression, lob/slice/smash mix, serve spin/timing window/pace/risk, planning interval. Six playing **styles** modify these (net player, retriever, power, all-rounder, wild, counter-puncher) and 16 named career characters use them.

Key behaviours:
- **Ball reading**: `predictBall` (full physics incl. walls) every few ticks; the bot goes to **the first point of the flight it can actually reach**, including after a wall rebound. Overheads before the bounce for players who dare.
- **Shot choice**: target x from a score of open space (net players weigh more), change of direction and avoidance of the dead centre; then lob / dejada / chiquita / slice / drive / smash / bandeja / vibora by situation; occasional "por 4" attempts.
- **Doubles positioning model (rebuilt after measuring)**: a team is in mode `D` (both defend from the baseline) or `A` (both at the net), decided once per tick from shared information (incoming lob or deep ball -> D; short ball -> A; our deep lob or short low ball -> A; a poor lob -> D), with hysteresis. One-up-one-back only at the serve and return. Side by side, shifting with the ball. **With a human partner the bot mirrors the human's line** and does not steal balls (needs a 0.9 m advantage).
- Serve: waits for the same narrow bounce window (tighter at higher levels), 2+ s before serving, softer than full pace, avoids the extreme corners.
- Bots tire like humans and save their sprint (>30 stamina).

**Analysis harness results (simulation, bot vs bot)**

| metric | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| pair split one-up-one-back | 58-62% | about 19% |
| both at the net | 6% | about 27% |
| long points with a player glued to the baseline | 15-31% | 0-3% (Pro/Legend) |
| points lost where nobody even swung | 95% | ~15% |
| average rally (Rookie/Club/Pro/Legend) | 4 / 7 / 11 / 31 | about 7 / 8 / 11 / 14 |
| aces (bot vs bot) | 13-26% | 0-3% |
| "por 4" winners per 100 points (Legend / Pro / Club) | n/a | 9 / 5 / 1 |

Level separation over 20 matches each: Legend beat Pro 14/20, Pro beat Club 18/20, Club beat Rookie 17/20.

Bugs found by that analysis (worth knowing about because similar ones may remain): bots lost the target they chose when starting a swing (later inputs were blank) so every shot went to the centre; slices clipped the net because net-clearance and landing correction were solved separately; players never recovered stamina between points; a "flub" meant the bot watched the ball go by and its partner never took over.

## 7. Game modes and meta

- **Quick match** (matchmaking), **private rooms** with friends, **practice 2v2 / 1v1** against bots (solo matches run in the browser, no network).
- **Career v2** (`shared/career2.js`): 3 seasons, 12 events (knockout draws of 4/8, group + final), real brackets (the matches the player does not play are simulated headlessly with the same engine and bots), 16 named characters, ranking points vs roster points, objectives (unlock two rackets), versioned save that migrates the old save, a short story scene before and after each event.
- **Training drills** (5): serve, forehand corner, lob, volley, smash; 3 stars; they run on the real game.
- **Player upgrades**: 10 attributes (speed, power, stamina, groundstroke, volley, smash, lob, reaction, defence, variety), up to level 10 (about +15%). Earned by wins (career, practice vs Club/Pro/Legend), drills and objectives. Apply to every solo match, **never online** (online everyone is equal).
- **Match history** stored in the browser (last 100 matches) with win rate, streak, best rally.
- Equipment: 4 archetypes, 7 hairs, 5 skins, 8 cosmetic rackets (two unlocked by objectives).

## 8. Client: controls, camera, UI, audio, visuals

- **Controls** (`input.js`): WASD/arrows move, Space hits (to serve: hold to charge, release to hit), G/Enter globo, R slice, Q soft (chiquita/dejada/vibora), E controlled (block/bandeja), Shift sprint, X jump, Tab stats, Esc pause, 1-4 quick chat, **M toggles mouse aim** (cursor places the target ring, click hits), **C cycles cameras**. Aim: while swinging you are planted and the direction held at contact decides the shot (left/right = side, forward = deep, back = short), like a tennis game. Remappable bindings, gamepad support (**not verified on a real gamepad**), swap-on-conflict, versioned persistence.
- **Cameras**: player (behind), first (over the shoulder), wall (low behind the glass), corner (high from a back corner), side (TV side line), broadcast, tactical (overhead), custom. (The last four fixed cameras were just added and have **not** been visually verified yet.)
- **Timing aid**: a circle that closes onto the ball (the owner asked for this); PERFECT/GOOD/LATE labels; reach ring; aim reticle with an accuracy ring.
- **Instant replays** after each point in solo matches (slow motion, high TV camera). Not online (the server cannot be paused).
- **HUD**: broadcast-style scoreboard (serving ball, set columns, games, points, game/set/match point flags), rally length chip after each point, stamina bar (amber when tired, red when exhausted), serve charge bar.
- **Visuals**: procedural jointed athletes (kinetic-chain swing keyframes per shot, run cycle, idle, emotes, footsteps), procedural rackets, glass/mesh/net, 4 venues with instanced crowds, ACES tone mapping, PCF soft shadows, optional bloom, auto quality drop if fps < 38.
- **Audio** (`audio.js`, all synthesised with Web Audio, no files): a distinct sound per shot type, glass vs mesh, footsteps/skids from the animation, per-venue reverb and ambience, optional spoken score calls (speech synthesis), **generative music** (pad, bass, electric-piano melody, light beat in matches).
- UI: home, lobby, results, settings, controls, equipment with a 3D preview, career hub/calendar/bracket/training/upgrades, history, quick-match search, pause menu as a hub (settings stay open while paused).

## 9. Development history (in order, with the owner's feedback)

1. Initial build to a master spec: authoritative server, rooms, scoring, bots, Three.js client, tests.
2. The owner asked for better graphics/physics, "realistic" player motion, and the padel shots (slice, bandeja, globo, vibora).
3. Controls without a mouse (Space hits, arrows move), a "closing circle" timing aid, 2K-tennis-style aim (planted while swinging, direction at contact).
4. A real public link (tunnels were unstable; moved to Render, Frankfurt for latency); a server crash fixed.
5. Solo matches moved into the browser because online lag was bad; 1v1 on a narrow fully-walled court; bot difficulty levels; career mode; public rooms.
6. A very large "PADEL CLUB 2.0" overhaul spec (physics/contact model, movement, animation, cameras, controls, career, courts, rackets, audio, VFX, UI, performance, multiplayer integrity, tests, GLB assets). Implemented in phases with an honest status list (`DEV_CHECKLIST.md`). GLB assets impossible without Blender; extra courts, music, off-court play were added later.
7. Spin/Magnus physics, racket contact model, rebuilt audio engine, remappable input/gamepad/mouse aim, camera presets.
8. Career v2, drills, attributes, objectives, rackets unlock.
9. **Off-court play** through side doors; ball physics pass; serve timing, aces fixed, chargeable serve; broadcast scoreboard; replays; music; rooftop venue; career story.
10. Owner's play-testing feedback fixes: mouse aim option back, rally length shown, pause freezes solo matches, doors visible and wider, ball coasts after the last point, match history, bots keep their role after the serve, stamina/fatigue and upgrades, glass vs fence walls and livelier floor, bots change direction in doubles, quick match with online count, contact pressure, lob quality, online pause by agreement, server planted on the line, settings usable while paused, **bot positioning rebuilt from a measured analysis**, positions restored after a fault, power serve can go long.
11. This turn (in progress): "por 4", wall-spin physics, more cameras (the owner defined "por 4" as a volley/smash that sends the ball completely out of the court).

Recurring complaints from the owner (themes): bots positioning and decisions; too many aces; aces/serves too easy or too fast; the AI always hitting through the middle; lobs spammed; things that look "unrealistic" (legs, physics); the game must be based on real padel; fatigue in long points; more wall play; more cameras.

## 10. Known weaknesses and honest limits

- Nothing here has been judged by a human on **real hardware**: gamepad, audio listening (music is synthesised, the owner said an earlier version was "very bad"; it was rewritten but not re-judged), mobile, low-end GPUs, a real 4-human session, the feel of the off-court doors.
- The in-app browser used for checking throttles `requestAnimationFrame` to about 1 fps, so visual checks were done with manual frame stepping and screenshots; real-time smoothness was never measured.
- No real 3D models (procedural only); animation is keyframe/IK-light, not motion capture; no cloth/hair.
- Bots are rule-based, not learned. The owner said bots "se colocan mal", "hacen cosas mal". The positioning was rebuilt from simulation metrics, but only measured bot-vs-bot, not against human playing styles.
- The "wall play" is physical (spin kick, two materials) but bots do not deliberately **play shots off the walls** (bajada de pared, doble pared, contrapared, chiquita to the glass), only react to rebounds. A defending bot does not choose wall plays.
- Doors/strip are narrow (1.8 m) and bots rarely produce door exits.
- The calibration constants (restitutions, friction, Magnus, drag, pace) were tuned by feel and simulation, not against measured ball data. I would like them reviewed against real padel ball physics (Penn/Head padel balls: pressure and bounce height standards, glass restitution, turf friction).
- The career has no cutscenes; story is text only. The career economy (coins) has nothing to buy yet.
- No accounts: history, career and upgrades live in the browser's local storage (per device); only profile XP is on the server.
- Online anti-cheat is basic (the server validates inputs, but a client could send maximum serve charge every time).

## 11. Ideas I have not done (candidates)

Real wall play for bots and a "wall play" teaching drill; lob/chiquita/dejada defence AI; a coaching/analysis mode that shows where you lose points; ELO/ranked online with accounts; tournaments online; spectator mode; replays online with a replay buffer on the server; cosmetics shop using coins; real GLB assets and animation retargeting; better crowd/stadium; more drills and a tutorial; accessibility options; localisation (Spanish UI); mobile/touch controls; performance profile and a benchmark harness; a determinism/desync test for client prediction; telemetry for balance.

## 12. How it is verified (so you can ask for more of the same)

`npm test` runs 123 tests: scoring, rules, physics and spin, shot solver, wall materials and spin kick, lob quality, contact pressure, "por 4", serve timing and power, doors and off-court movement, bot positioning metrics, bot level ordering, career v2 brackets and save migration, drills, quick match and pause (including real WebSocket clients and fake clocks), match history. Simulation scripts (not committed) measured bot-vs-bot statistics (aces, rally lengths, pair positions, why points are lost).

## 13. Files worth reading first

`shared/physics.js` (ball, walls, player movement), `shared/game.js` (rules, contact, shots, serve), `shared/ai.js` (bots), `server/rooms.js` (matches, pause), `client/js/game.js` (prediction, cameras, replay), `DEV_CHECKLIST.md` (honest status of every feature).

## 14. The answer I want from you

Reply with **one prompt**, in English, ready to paste into Claude Code, that:

1. Starts with a short role/context paragraph (it may assume Claude Code can read the repository and this briefing is the owner's summary).
2. Contains a **prioritised list of 8 to 12 improvements** that would most raise quality, realism and fun, each with: the problem, the concrete change, files likely involved, and **measurable acceptance criteria** (for example "average rally 10-16 shots for Pro in 20 simulated matches", "bots choose a wall play in at least 10% of defensive situations", "a unit test that ...").
3. Pays special attention to: (a) **real padel realism** of the ball and wall physics and calibration against real data; (b) **bot tactics**, especially wall play and doubles teamwork; (c) feel and readability of the game for a human (timing, camera, feedback); (d) things I cannot verify myself and how to instrument them (telemetry, debug overlays, benchmark scripts).
4. Says clearly what **not** to do (do not rewrite the architecture, keep the shared deterministic code, keep tests green, no new build step, no external assets that cannot be generated).
5. Ends with instructions to work in small verified steps, run `npm test` after each, update `DEV_CHECKLIST.md` honestly (done / partly / not done), and commit with clear messages.

Be specific and sceptical. If something in this document looks physically or tactically wrong for real padel, say so in the prompt and tell Claude Code how to correct it.

===== FILE: DEV_CHECKLIST.md =====
# PADEL CLUB 2.0: development checklist (honest status)

Legend: ✅ done and tested · 🟡 partly done · ❌ not started

## Assets and visuals
- ✅ 4 character archetypes with distinct silhouettes (height, shoulders, build, limbs), blue and red kits, 7 hair styles, 5 skin tones, shirt textures, shoes, wristbands, numbers: `client/js/characters.js` (procedural meshes, not GLB: there is no Blender in this environment)
- ✅ 8 padel racket models (teardrop / round / diamond heads, bevelled frame, real see-through perforations, throat bridge, wrapped grip, cord): `buildRacket()`
- ✅ Ball model with seams, spin-driven rotation and trail
- ✅ 3 environments: Premium Indoor Club, Mediterranean Court (outdoor), Night Arena: `client/js/envs.js`
- ✅ Court selection (lobby for hosts, home screen for solo, career stages carry a court); server validates and sends the court to all clients
- ✅ Equipment screen with live 3D preview, persisted in the browser and sent to the server so other players see your look
- 🟡 Court surface physics: three documented surfaces (indoor/night fast, outdoor ~2-3% slower bounce and speed). Glass/mesh acoustics are not modelled.
- ❌ GLB/glTF pipeline, texture compression, level of detail, loading screen with progress

## Animation
- ✅ Jointed rig, shot-specific keyframes, kinetic chain, split step, run cycle, braking lean, landing, celebration/disappointment, ball tracking
- 🟡 Racket contact alignment: contact keyframe sits in the server's contact window; the hit is still decided by the server and the animation is not re-timed to the exact contact tick
- ❌ Skeletal clips, inverse kinematics for feet and hands

## Physics and gameplay
- ✅ Spin (Magnus), drag, spin-dependent bounces, shot solver that compensates spin and drag, per-shot spin and speed profiles
- ✅ Racket contact model (shared/game.js): swing timing sets the face angle (open = higher, slower arc + backspin; closed = flatter + topspin, no net help when very closed); distance from the sweet spot stands for the point on the face (edge hits lose pace and scatter); sideways running costs accuracy, moving into the shot adds pace; the racket meets the ball at the sweet spot instead of the first instant it is in reach. Labels: perfect / clean / early / late / off-centre / poor. Tests in test/contact.test.js
- 🟡 It is a mathematical impact model driven by timing and distance, not a simulation of the racket's real orientation in 3D
- 🟡 Aiming error now depends on contact quality, edge contact and running; the timing window is the same for every shot type
- ❌ Collision sub-stepping / CCD (the 60 Hz step has not shown tunnelling in tests)
- ✅ Off-court play (doubles): access doors in the side walls near the net (1.4–3.0 m from it, 2 m high) lead to a fenced strip along each side. After the first bounce a ball may leave through a door; the receiving team follows it, may let it bounce once outside, and must return it along the strip, round the net post and in through the other door so it lands in the opponents' court (hitting the glass, or landing outside, loses the point). A ball leaving without bouncing is out. Players and bots can walk out only through the doors; 1v1 stays fully walled. Unit-tested (physics, rules, movement, bots)
- 🟡 Off-court play has not been played by a person: bots rarely produce door exits on their own, and the strip is narrow (1.8 m)
- ❌ Stamina effects beyond sprint, player-player collisions beyond teammate separation

## Controls
- ✅ Input module (client/js/input.js): keyboard, mouse buttons and gamepad become actions; 4 schemes (Classic keyboard, Keyboard + mouse aim, Gamepad, Custom); per-device bindings; rebinding with press-to-assign, Esc cancels, duplicate detection resolved by swapping; restore defaults; live input test; controller diagram showing the real mapping; dead zone, response curve, hold/toggle sprint, aim marker on/off; versioned persistence with safe fallback
- ✅ Mouse aiming: ray from the camera onto the court; ring marks the target area and widens when the ball is far from the sweet spot (it is an area, not a guarantee). Off by default: the mouse is never required
- ✅ Right stick (or left stick) places the shot; keyboard steering is resolved by the server at contact
- 🟡 Tested automatically: bindings, conflicts, persistence, corrupt storage, pad reading, capture. Checked in the browser with a SIMULATED gamepad and simulated key events: NOT with a physical controller
- ❌ Mouse wheel actions, pointer-lock cursor capture, separate per-player profiles in one browser, camera presets, power indicator, aim assist

## Career
- ✅ Career v2 (shared/career2.js, client/js/career-ui.js): 3 seasons and 12 events (knockout draws of 4/8, group + final), per-event court and rules, real brackets (matches you do not play are simulated headlessly with the same engine and bots), 16 named characters with playing styles, world ranking vs roster points, objectives (rewards granted once, unlock two rackets), versioned save (padel.career2) that migrates the old ladder save without deleting it
- ✅ Training: 5 drills run on the real game (serve, forehand corner, lob, volley, smash), 3 stars each, development points only for improvement
- ✅ Development: 9 attributes, each level a small bonus (about +1%), applied only to local matches, never online
- ✅ Story: a short scene before and after each of the 12 events (a character speaks; text only, no cutscenes)
- 🟡 Unit-tested logic; only played through in the in-app browser with scripted input, not by a person

## Camera
- ✅ Presets: player, broadcast, tactical, custom (distance, height, smoothing, field of view); optional drift toward the ball; the camera never rotates with the movement keys

## Audio
- ✅ Rewritten engine (client/js/audio.js): a distinct sound per shot type, character changes with contact quality, glass vs mesh, footsteps and skids from the animation, per-court room reverb and ambience (hall / wind and birds / crowd and insects), random variation and rate limits, separate effects / environment / announcer volumes, optional spoken score calls (browser speech synthesis)
- 🟡 All sounds are still synthesised (no recorded samples are shipped). Not listened to by a person in this session: only checked that nothing throws
- ✅ Music: a generative synthesised loop (pad, bass, arpeggio; a light beat in matches), own volume in Settings. Not listened to by a person, and it is synthesised, not composed
- ❌ Recorded effort/celebration sounds, crowd announcements

## Multiplayer, deployment
- ✅ 4-player authoritative rooms, reconnect, 60 Hz snapshots, looks and court synchronised (tests)
- ✅ `render.yaml` targets Render's Frankfurt (EU) region. A service's region cannot be changed once created: recreate the service from the Blueprint.

## Tests
- 97 automated tests: scoring, rules, physics/spin, contact model, singles, career v2 (brackets, migration, rewards), drills, input, looks/courts, 4 real WebSocket clients.
- Manually checked in the in-app browser: menu, equipment screen, the three courts (menu background), a night-arena practice match.
- NOT checked: gamepad, audio, a real 4-human session, mobile, performance on low-end GPUs.

## Ball physics (latest pass)
- ✅ Quadratic air drag on top of linear drag, speed-dependent glass restitution, much deader mesh above 3 m, speed-dependent floor bounce, net-tape cord (pops over with lost pace) instead of always stopping at the tape

## AI, serves, replays, scoreboard (latest pass)
- ✅ Serve timing: the bounce is judged ~0.05 s after the press and only a narrow window around waist height gives a good serve (faster and more accurate); a closing ring shows it (Timing indicator setting). Bots wait for the same window, a tighter one at higher levels, and receivers react to the serve instead of anticipating it
- ✅ Bug fixed: perfectly struck slices and other shots could clip the net (net clearance and landing correction are now solved together)
- ✅ Bots now attack high balls (smash, bandeja, víbora), lob less, and the levels are separated more clearly (checked by simulation: Legend beats Pro, Pro beats Club, Club beats Rookie)
- ✅ Instant replay after each point in solo matches (slow motion, high TV camera, Space/Esc to skip, toggle in Settings). NOT in online matches: the server keeps running, so there is no safe moment to pause
- ✅ Broadcast-style scoreboard: serving ball, set columns, games and points, animated changes, game/set/match point flags

## Courts
- ✅ Fourth court: Sunset Rooftop (skyline with lit windows, string lights, plants, city ambience, slightly quick turf). Selectable on the home screen and in lobbies. A fifth (resort) is not made

## Fixes after play-testing
- ✅ Mouse aim is back as a visible option: Settings, the pause menu, or the M key (target ring follows the cursor, left click hits, right click = globo)
- ✅ Rally length shown after every point (chip under the scoreboard and in the banner)
- ✅ Pausing a solo match now freezes everything (logic and animation). Online matches cannot be paused: the pause menu says so
- ✅ After the last point the ball keeps coasting instead of freezing mid-air
- ✅ Doors are wider (2 m) and marked: glowing frames, lit lintel and a floor mat inside the court
- ✅ Music rewritten (warm pad, soft electric-piano melody, swing, reverb, softer beat). Still synthesised and not judged by a person
- ✅ Match history: every finished match (practice, career, online) is saved in this browser (last 100) and shown under History on the home screen with wins, win rate, streak, best rally and aces. It lives in the browser's storage, so it is per device and is lost if site data is cleared; it is not synced to the server
- ✅ Bots hold their positions after the serve: roles (net / baseline) are fixed when the point starts and only swap after a real lob chase, so the server and receiver stay near the baseline instead of drifting up to the net; default positions are deeper (baseline 7.6 m, net 3.4 m). Checked by simulation (levels still ordered: Legend > Pro > Club > Rookie)

## Upgrades and fatigue
- ✅ Fatigue: stamina drains 1.5/s while the ball is in play even if you stand still (sprinting costs extra) and only recovers between points, so long points wear you down. Below 45 stamina you run up to 18% slower, hit with less accuracy and pace, and serve less accurately (everyone, bots too, online as well). The bar turns amber (tired) and red (exhausted). Bug fixed on the way: players did not recover stamina between points before
- ✅ Upgrades: 10 attributes (new Power; Speed +1.5% per level, Stamina = slower fatigue and faster recovery, plus accuracy, spin, reaction…), up to level 10 (about +15%). Available from the Upgrades button on the home screen and the Career > Development tab; they apply to every solo match and never online. Points come from career wins/titles, drills, objectives and practice wins against Club, Pro and Legend bots (+1/+2/+3; nothing against Rookies)

## Serves and aces (latest pass)
- ✅ Bug fixed: bots lost the target they chose when they started a swing (their later inputs were blank), so every bot shot, serves included, went to the default spot (short serves = many aces). They now keep the aim until contact
- ✅ Bot receivers anticipate: they go to the first point of the ball's flight they can actually reach (including after a wall rebound). Aces in bot-vs-bot play dropped from 13–26% to 0–3%
- ✅ Bots wait longer before serving (2+ s, random) so receivers are set, serve with less than full pace and avoid the extreme corners every time
- ✅ Serve: the bounce of the ball in the server's hand is slower (1.1 s per bounce instead of 0.8), and holding the hit key charges serve power (tap = normal, full charge 0.9 s = +22% pace but a less accurate serve); the serve is struck when you release
- ✅ Bot levels retuned after these changes (Legend 9/12 vs Pro, Pro 10/12 vs Club, Club 10/12 vs Rookie in simulation)

## Ball and walls, bot targets (latest pass)
- ✅ Two kinds of wall: glass (back walls up to 3 m, and the back 4 m of the side walls; all of the side walls in 1v1) is hard and lively (restitution 0.82, keeps its speed); the fence/verja (the middle of the side walls and the mesh above 3 m) is soft and dead (0.34, much more friction). The rebound sound uses the real material. Unit-tested
- ✅ Livelier floor: restitution raised (0.85 indoor/night, 0.83 outdoor, 0.84 rooftop) and less loss on hard impacts, so the ball bounces higher and rallies use the walls more
- ✅ Bot targets: in doubles bots now weigh the open space (net players count more), changing the direction of the ball and avoiding the dead centre, so they play cross-court and down the line instead of always back through the middle (landing spots in simulation are about 15% central, the rest spread to both sides)
- Bot levels retuned again after the physics change (Legend 13/16 vs Pro, Pro 15/16 vs Club, Club 15/16 vs Rookie; aces 2–9%)

## Quick match, pressure, lobs, positioning (latest pass)
- ✅ Quick match: a button on the home screen puts you in a queue; four players are matched into a doubles game, and after 10 s anyone still waiting plays with bots filling the empty seats. Shows the queue size and how many players are online (also shown on the home button, refreshed every second); cancellable. Server module server/quick.js, tested with real WebSocket clients and with a fake clock. The first two players matched are team-mates
- ✅ Pressure on contact (everyone: bots, humans, online): a ball reached at full stretch, hit with great pace, or taken on the run is answered with a weaker, shorter, less accurate shot ("Stretched" label), so a good volley or placed ball wins the exchange. A poorly struck ball also carries less pace and falls shorter
- ✅ Lobs: quality depends on contact. A clean lob climbs high and lands deep; a poor one is low and short (about 2.2+ m apex), easy to smash
- ✅ Doubles positioning: bots choose a stance (both back when a lob comes or the rivals attack, both at the net when the rivals are pinned back, otherwise one up one back), move as a pair keeping their width, and shift toward the ball

## Online pause, fixed server (latest pass)
- ✅ Online pause: the pause menu has "Request a pause". The other humans get an Accept / Decline prompt (10 s to answer). When everybody accepts the match freezes for at most 30 s, or until every human presses Resume; a disconnected player does not block it; each player has two pauses per match; with only bots around a pause starts at once. Server-authoritative (Room.pauseCtl), tested with fake clocks and over real WebSockets. Solo matches still pause instantly
- ✅ The server stays planted on the line from the start of the point until the serve is hit (no movement at all), so the serve position is the same every time
- ✅ The pause menu is a hub: Settings, Controls and the rest open on top of it and closing them returns to the pause menu (the match stays paused); only Resume resumes. While paused the view still follows the settings (camera preset, ball size, graphics), so you can preview changes

## Bot positioning, analysed and retrained (latest pass)
Method: a simulation harness measured how bot pairs really move and why they lose points, against how real pairs play.
- Found: 58-62% of the time bot pairs were split one-up-one-back (real pairs are mostly on the same line), both-at-the-net only 6%, and 15-31% of long points had a player glued to the baseline. Worse: in 95% of the points a bot team lost, NOBODY had even swung (the bot that "flubbed" a ball just watched it, and its partner never took over), and 60% of those balls were within reach
- ✅ New doubles model: the pair is on the same line almost always: both defend (baseline) or both attack (net), decided once per tick per team from shared information (incoming lob/deep ball → back; short ball → up; our deep lob or short low ball → up; our poor lob → back). One-up-one-back only at the serve and the return. Side by side, shifting with the ball, each keeping their own side; the player on the ball side takes it. Now (hard): both net 27%, both back 34%, split 19%, nobody glued to either end
- ✅ Mishits instead of passive misses: a flubbing bot still goes for the ball and strikes it badly (into the net or long), so points end in errors rather than in free winners; rallies are now 6.5 / 9.6 / 15.6 / 25 shots for Rookie / Club / Pro / Legend
- ✅ With a person as partner the bot follows THEIR line (up when you are up, back when you are back; back to cover a lob) and does not steal balls that are yours (needs a clear 0.9 m advantage)
- Levels after retuning (20 matches each): Legend beat Pro 15/20, Pro beat Club 16/20, Club beat Rookie 17/20, Legend beat Rookie 20/20
- ✅ After a fault everybody (server, partner, receivers) returns to their starting place for the second serve
- ✅ Serve power has a price: a full charge (+22% pace) tends to fly long past the service line (a clean contact keeps it closer, a mistimed one sails); a tap or bot serve is unaffected

## Wall play, "por 4", cameras (latest pass)
- ✅ "Por 4": a clean short smash from near the net comes down steeply with topspin and bounces out of the court over the fence (winner, "¡Por 4!"); bots go for it with a probability tied to their smash skill. Humans: smash while holding down (short) with a clean contact
- ✅ Wall spin: topspin climbs the glass, backspin drops the ball dead, the wall strips part of the spin
- ✅ Four new cameras (first, wall, corner, side) and C to cycle them. NOT visually verified yet
- 🟡 Bots still react to wall rebounds rather than choosing wall plays on purpose

## The ball (latest pass)
- ✅ Model: felt texture with fibres, bump map and the curved seam of a real ball (512 px texture, 32x24 sphere)
- ✅ Spin that follows the physics: topspin/backspin turn about the axis across its path (the right way round), sidespin about the vertical, rolling on the floor; speed streak (a tapered tail) on fast balls; squash on every impact (floor and walls); a scuff mark where it bounced; a darker, sharper shadow near the floor and a faint plumb line from the ball to its shadow when it is high (readable height from any camera)
- ✅ Physics: a ball resting on the floor now rolls and slows gently (it used to be hit by the bounce friction every tick, stopping dead and emitting a bounce event each tick); tested
- Seen in one browser screenshot only; not judged in real-time play

===== FILE: README.md =====
# PADEL CLUB

Browser-based 4-player online padel. A Node server runs the match (authoritative physics, rules and scoring);
browsers are thin clients that render with Three.js and send inputs.

## Run it

```bash
npm install
npm start            # http://localhost:3000
npm test             # 30 tests: scoring, rules, physics, and 4 real WebSocket clients
```

Requires Node 20+. No build step: the client is plain ES modules served by the same Node process.

## Play with three friends

1. One person opens the game, clicks **Play with friends**. A room code and invite link appear.
2. Friends open the link (or type the code under *Join room*). Everyone lands in a seat; click **Sit here** or let the host **Move** people between Team A and Team B.
3. Non-hosts press **I am ready**; the host presses **Start match**.
4. For friends on other networks the server must be reachable by them: see *Deployment*, or tunnel your local port (e.g. `cloudflared tunnel --url http://localhost:3000`).

**1 vs 1**: in the lobby set *Mode* to *1 vs 1* (host). Two seats on a narrower 7 m court closed by glass walls on all sides (the ball plays off the glass like in doubles); bots fill the free seat.

**Career**: three seasons of tournaments (knockout draws and group stages, singles and doubles) against 16 named characters with their own playing styles. The matches you do not play are simulated with the same engine, so brackets and the world ranking are real. Earn ranking points, coins and development points; complete objectives to unlock rackets; use the Training tab for drills and the Development tab to improve nine attributes (small bonuses, solo only). Progress is saved in your browser.

**Off-court play (2v2)**: each side wall has two access doors near the net. If the ball bounces and then leaves through a door, chase it outside and return it along the strip and in through the other door.

**Match history**: every finished match is remembered in your browser (History button on the home screen).

**Bot levels**: Rookie, Club, Pro, Legend (pick them on the home screen, in the lobby, or via the career stage). Higher levels react faster, cover more ground, aim for open space and the lines, and mix globos, dejadas, slices and spin serves.

**Public rooms**: the host can set *Room visibility → Public* in the lobby; open lobbies appear under *Rooms* on the home screen for one-click joining.

**Courts and equipment**: three environments (Premium Indoor Club, Mediterranean Court, Night Arena), chosen by the host in the lobby or on the home screen for solo play; the turf differs slightly (outdoor is a few percent slower). *Equipment* lets you pick one of 4 player archetypes, hair, skin and one of 8 cosmetic rackets, with a live 3D preview. Your look is shown to the other players. See DEV_CHECKLIST.md for what is and is not done.

**Cameras**: Settings has four presets (player, broadcast, tactical, custom). **Racket contact**: the face angle comes from swing timing and the strike point from the distance to the sweet spot, so timing and positioning change the arc, spin, pace and accuracy of each shot. **Audio**: distinct shot sounds, court-specific rooms and ambience, optional announcer.

**Practice vs AI** starts a room with you and three bots. Empty seats are only ever filled by clearly labelled AI.

### Controls
Open **Settings → Controls → Configure** to pick a scheme (Classic keyboard, Keyboard + mouse aim, Gamepad, Custom), rebind every action (keyboard, mouse or gamepad), tune the stick, and test your inputs live. Defaults:

| Input | Action |
|---|---|
| W A S D | Move (camera-relative; W = toward the net) |
| Space | Hit / serve (press just before the ball reaches the ring around your feet) |
| Arrow keys | Move (same as WASD). You are planted while swinging, and the direction you hold at contact is where the ball goes (left/right, up = deep, down = short); the mouse does not aim (left click hits, right click = globo) |
| G / Enter | Globo (lob) |
| R | Slice: backspin, skids low and slow |
| Q | Chiquita (near net) / dejada (from deep) / **víbora** on overheads |
| E | Block on low balls / **bandeja** on overheads |
| X / Shift | Jump / sprint (stamina) |
| Tab / Esc / 1–4 | Stats / menu / quick chat |

Shot type is chosen from context (ball height, distance to the net, ball side, modifiers): forehand (topspin), backhand, slice, volley, globo, remate (smash), bandeja, víbora, chiquita, dejada, block, serve.

### Ball physics
The ball has real spin: Magnus lift/dive (backspin floats, topspin dives), sidespin curves, air drag, and spin-dependent bounces. Backspin gives a low, slow bounce; topspin kicks forward; víbora sidespin kicks sideways off the glass. The shot solver simulates the real physics and corrects the launch, so the landing spot you aim at stays accurate whatever the spin. Mishit balls carry less spin.

## Architecture

```
shared/    Pure simulation, imported by BOTH server and client
  constants.js  court/physics/player tuning
  physics.js    stepPlayer (also used for client prediction), stepBall, ballistic shot solver
  scoring.js    explicit scoring state machine (points, deuce/advantage/golden point, games, sets, tiebreaks, service order)
  game.js       PadelGame: players, shots, contact quality, padel rules (bounces, glass, net, serve box, faults), stats
  ai.js         doubles bot: predicts the ball, covers its half, picks lobs/smashes/bandejas
server/
  index.js      HTTP static + WebSocket server, fixed 60 Hz loop, rate limiting
  rooms.js      Room lifecycle (lobby → ready → match → results → rematch), reconnect, bot takeover
  store.js      File-backed persistence (guest profiles, XP, match history)
client/
  js/net.js     WebSocket + auto-reconnect, guest token
  js/game.js    input, prediction + reconciliation, snapshot interpolation, camera, event feedback
  js/scene.js   court, glass/mesh, net, procedural player models and animation, ball
  js/ui.js      menus, lobby, HUD, results, settings
  js/audio.js   synthesised sound (no audio files)
```

### Networking model
- Server ticks at 60 Hz and broadcasts compact snapshots at 30 Hz (ball, 4 players); discrete events (hit, bounce, fault, point) are sent reliably.
- Clients send timestamped inputs (`seq`, move axes, aim point, swing/lob/jump/modifier flags). The server validates and clamps everything. It decides every contact, bounce and point; a client cannot submit scores, ball positions or results (unknown messages are ignored; covered by a test).
- The local player is predicted with the same `stepPlayer` and reconciled against the server's acked `seq`. Other players and the ball are interpolated ~100 ms behind.
- Disconnects: a **bot takes over the seat instantly** (the point continues, nobody is stuck waiting); reconnecting with the same guest token returns control. In the lobby a seat is held for 60 s. Rooms with no connected players are removed after 10 minutes.
- Persistence: when the server's own simulation ends a match it records the result, grants XP, and updates profiles.

## What works / what does not (honest status)

Working and covered by tests or verified in a browser:
- Private rooms with codes and invite links, 4 seats, teams, host moves players, ready check, host-controlled format (1 or 3 sets, 2/4/6 games, golden point, AI level), chat + quick chat, rematch without a new room, reconnect.
- Authoritative doubles match with real padel rules and scoring (see `test/`), serve box, faults/double faults, glass rebounds after the first bounce, net, out calls.
- Practice mode vs bots; bots cover for disconnected players.
- Guest profiles with level/XP/win-loss/history (file store); real stats (winners, errors, aces, smashes, volleys, longest rally) from match events.
- Settings: graphics quality, volumes, camera distance/height, ball size, timing indicator, high contrast, fullscreen.

Not built yet (intentionally not faked):
- Public matchmaking / Quick Play, ranked mode, ratings, leaderboards.
- Friends list, presence, voice chat, emotes beyond quick chat, accounts (guests only).
- True racket-face physics: spin is chosen per shot type (and scaled by contact quality), not computed from the racket angle.
- Control remapping, gamepad, training drills (use Practice vs AI), beginner doubles hints, replay/tactical camera.
- Only one environment (indoor club). Players are procedurally animated jointed figures (elbows, knees, torso twist, split-step, head tracking the ball), not imported skeletal models.
- Persistence is a JSON file, not PostgreSQL (`server/store.js` is the only file to replace).
- Not yet load-tested; the game loop is single-process, one Node process hosts all rooms.

## Manual 4-player test checklist
Use four browsers/devices (or three browser profiles plus one incognito window). Replace `HOST` with the host's address.
1. Player 1: open `http://HOST:3000`, enter a name, **Play with friends**. Note the code.
2. Players 2–4: open the invite link. Expect all four names in the lobby, the 5th person gets "Room is full".
3. Host moves someone to the other team; all screens update within a second. Players 2–4 ready up; **Start** stays disabled until they do.
4. Play a point: all four see the same ball path, the same call and the same score.
5. Hit the ball into the glass before it bounces → "Out". Let it bounce twice → point for the hitter. Hit the net → "Net".
6. Close one tab mid-point, reopen the site (same browser): you rejoin the same seat; meanwhile a bot covered.
7. Finish a match (use *Games per set = 2*, *Single set*): results screen identical on all clients; **Rematch** restarts without a new room; profile shows XP and history.
8. Throttle one browser (DevTools → Network → Slow 3G) and confirm it stays playable and others are unaffected.

## Deployment
Single Node service + a persistent disk. **Region:** the Blueprint targets Frankfurt (EU). Render cannot change the region of an existing service, so delete the old service and create it again from the Blueprint.
- **Docker** (any container host, Fly.io, Railway, Render, a VPS): `docker build -t padel-club . && docker run -p 3000:3000 -v padel-data:/data padel-club`
- Put it behind HTTPS (the client automatically uses `wss://` on `https://` pages). Your proxy must allow WebSocket upgrades on `/ws` and keep idle connections open > 30 s.
- Environment: see `.env.example` (`PORT`, `DATA_DIR`). No secrets are required.
- Scale-out: rooms live in memory in one process. To scale horizontally, shard by room code at the load balancer (sticky routing) and move `Store` to a shared database.
===== END FILE: README.md =====

===== FILE: shared/ai.js =====
// Padel AI. Produces the same input a human client would send, so bots obey every game rule.
// Used for practice, career mode, and to cover for players who disconnect mid-match.
//
// Four levels (the ids are what rooms and the career use):
//   easy = Rookie   normal = Club   hard = Pro   expert = Legend

import { clamp, COURT } from './constants.js';
import { predictBall, serveBallHeight } from './physics.js';
import { STYLE_MODS } from './roster.js';

export const BOT_LEVELS = ['easy', 'normal', 'hard', 'expert'];
export const BOT_LABELS = { easy: 'Rookie', normal: 'Club', hard: 'Pro', expert: 'Legend' };

const SKILL = {
  // speed: top speed factor · react: seconds before reacting · noise: aim scatter · reach: swing distance
  // flub: chance to miss a ball outright · aggr: willingness to attack · lob/slice/smash: shot mix
  // serveSpin: chance of a spin serve · serveTiming: waits for the ideal bounce height · plan: replanning interval
  easy: { speed: 0.9, sprint: true, react: 0.3, noise: 2.0, reach: 1.9, flub: 0.3, timingErr: 0.24, aggr: 0.12, lob: 0.35, slice: 0.08, smash: 0.35, serveSpin: 0, serveTiming: false, serveWindow: 0.45, servePace: 0.68, serveRisk: 0.0, plan: 0.12 },
  normal: { speed: 0.96, sprint: true, react: 0.18, noise: 1.4, reach: 1.95, flub: 0.24, timingErr: 0.14, aggr: 0.3, lob: 0.45, slice: 0.18, smash: 0.5, serveSpin: 0.15, serveTiming: false, serveWindow: 0.25, servePace: 0.72, serveRisk: 0.3, plan: 0.1 },
  hard: { speed: 0.95, sprint: true, react: 0.09, noise: 0.5, reach: 2.2, flub: 0.18, timingErr: 0.07, aggr: 0.5, lob: 0.5, slice: 0.25, smash: 0.7, serveSpin: 0.4, serveTiming: true, serveWindow: 0.12, servePace: 0.76, serveRisk: 0.6, plan: 0.07 },
  expert: { speed: 1.1, sprint: true, react: 0.03, noise: 0.15, reach: 2.4, flub: 0.15, timingErr: 0.03, aggr: 0.45, lob: 0.55, slice: 0.3, smash: 0.85, serveSpin: 0.6, serveTiming: true, serveWindow: 0.05, servePace: 0.8, serveRisk: 0.85, plan: 0.05 },
};

const skillCache = new Map();
/**
 * A bot is a level ('easy'..'expert') optionally with a style ({level, style}). The style shifts the level's tendencies:
 * where the bot stands (netZ/backZ) and how often it attacks, lobs, slices and smashes.
 */
export function skillFor(d) {
  const level = typeof d === 'object' && d ? d.level : d;
  const style = typeof d === 'object' && d ? d.style : null;
  const key = level + '|' + style;
  if (skillCache.has(key)) return skillCache.get(key);
  const base = { ...(SKILL[level] || SKILL.normal) };
  const m = STYLE_MODS[style] || {};
  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(m)) {
    if (k === 'netZ' || k === 'backZ') base[k] = v;
    else base[k] = (base[k] ?? 0) + v;
  }
  for (const k of ['aggr', 'lob', 'slice', 'smash', 'flub']) base[k] = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, base[k]));
  base.speed = Math.max(0.8, Math.min(1.1, base.speed));
  base.noise = Math.max(0.1, base.noise);
  skillCache.set(key, base);
  return base;
}

export function makeBotMemory() {
  return { serveT: 0, plan: null, planAge: 99, react: 0, lastTeam: -1, flub: false };
}

const blank = () => ({ mx: 0, mz: 0, sprint: false, swing: false, jump: false, ax: 0, az: 0, mod: 0, lob: false, keep: true });

function moveTo(inp, p, tx, tz, skill, allowSprint) {
  const dx = tx - p.x, dz = tz - p.z;
  const d = Math.hypot(dx, dz);
  if (d < 0.08) return d;
  const k = Math.min(1, d / 0.5) * skill.speed;
  inp.mx = (dx / d) * k;
  inp.mz = (dz / d) * k;
  inp.sprint = allowSprint && skill.sprint && d > 2.5 && p.stamina > 30; // bots save their legs too
  return d;
}

export function botInput(game, slot, mem, difficulty = 'normal', dt = 1 / 60) {
  const skill = skillFor(difficulty);
  const p = game.players[slot];
  const inp = blank();
  const os = p.team === 0 ? 1 : -1;
  const r = game.rally;
  const b = game.ball;
  const rnd = game.rng;

  if (game.phase === 'serve') {
    if (game.serverSlot === slot) serve(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem, dt);
    mem.plan = null; mem.react = 0; mem.lastTeam = -1;
    mem.role = Math.abs(p.z) < 5 ? 'net' : 'back'; // the server and the receiver start at the back, their partners at the net
    if (game.aiTeam) game.aiTeam[p.team] = { mode: 'D', since: game.time, tick: -1 };
    return inp;
  }
  if (game.phase !== 'rally') return inp;

  const incoming = r.lastTeam !== p.team;
  if (r.lastTeam !== mem.lastTeam) {
    mem.lastTeam = r.lastTeam; mem.planAge = 99;
    mem.react = r.serving ? skill.react * 0.6 : skill.react; // receivers do not anticipate: they react to the serve, not before it
    mem.flub = rnd() < skill.flub * (r.serving ? 0.35 : 1);
    mem.jit = (rnd() - 0.5) * skill.timingErr; // each level presses a little earlier or later
  }
  mem.react = Math.max(0, mem.react - dt);

  if (incoming && (b.ex || Math.abs(b.x) > COURT.HALF_W) && game.openings) {
    outsideBall(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem);
    return inp;
  }

  if (incoming) {
    mem.planAge += dt;
    if (mem.planAge > skill.plan) {
      mem.planAge = 0;
      const samples = predictBall(b, 3.0);
      let pick = null;
      let bounced = r.bounces >= 1;
      let first = null; // earliest playable point, used when nothing is reachable in time
      const canReach = (s) => Math.hypot(s.x - p.x, s.z - p.z) <= 0.6 + skill.speed * 6.4 * Math.max(0, s.t - mem.react);
      for (const s of samples) {
        if (s.floor) bounced = true;
        const mine = p.team === 0 ? s.z < -0.4 : s.z > 0.4;
        // overhead: a high ball falling in my half before it bounces is attacked (smash / bandeja) by players who dare
        const overhead = mine && !bounced && s.vy < 0 && s.y < 2.45 && s.y > 1.95 && Math.abs(s.z) < 7 && rnd() < 0.25 + skill.smash * 0.75;
        // groundstrokes after the bounce (also off the back wall); volleys only in the net zone
        const ground = mine && s.y < 1.7 && s.vy <= (bounced ? 2.0 : 0.5) && (bounced || Math.abs(s.z) < 4.2);
        if (!overhead && !ground) continue;
        first = first || s;
        if (canReach(s)) { pick = s; break; } // anticipation: go to the first point that can actually be reached, even after a wall rebound
      }
      pick = pick || first;
      mem.plan = pick || null;
    }
    const partner = game.players[slot ^ 1];
    const plan = mem.plan;
    let responsible = true;
    if (plan && partner.active) {
      const me = Math.hypot(plan.x - p.x, plan.z - p.z);
      const pa = Math.hypot(plan.x - partner.x, plan.z - partner.z);
      // the player on the side of the ball takes it; the middle goes to the nearer one
      const side = (plan.x - (p.x + partner.x) / 2) * (p.x < partner.x ? -1 : 1) > 0 ? -0.5 : 0.5;
      const margin = partner.human ? 0.9 : 0.3; // with a person as partner: do not steal their balls
      responsible = me + side < pa - margin || (!partner.human && Math.abs(me + side - pa) <= 0.3 && slot < partner.slot);
    }
    if (plan && responsible) {
      if (mem.react <= 0) moveTo(inp, p, plan.x, plan.z - os * (plan.y > 1.8 ? 0.3 : 0.75), skill, true);
    } else {
      formation(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem);
    }
    // swing when the ball is close enough and on the way
    const dx = b.x - p.x, dz = b.z - p.z;
    const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dz);
    const onMySide = p.team === 0 ? b.z < 0.2 : b.z > -0.2;
    if (responsible && onMySide && !p.swing && p.cd <= 0 && !r.serving
        && dist < skill.reach && b.y < 2.9 && b.y > 0.1 && mem.react <= 0 && swingNow(b, dx, dz, dist, mem)) {
      chooseShot(inp, game, p, os, skill);
      inp.swing = true;
      inp.slip = !!mem.flub; // a mishit: the bot still goes for the ball (it used to just watch it go by) but strikes it badly
    }
  } else {
    formation(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem);
  }
  return inp;
}

/** The ball left through a door: the nearer player of the receiving team follows it outside and returns it round the net post. */
function outsideBall(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem) {
  const b = game.ball;
  const partner = game.players[p.slot ^ 1];
  const mine = Math.hypot(b.x - p.x, b.z - p.z), theirs = partner.active ? Math.hypot(b.x - partner.x, b.z - partner.z) : Infinity;
  if (theirs < mine - 0.3 || (Math.abs(theirs - mine) <= 0.3 && partner.slot < p.slot)) { formation(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem); return; }
  const s = Math.sign(b.x) || 1;
  const doorZ = -os * (COURT.DOOR.z0 + COURT.DOOR.z1) / 2;
  if (Math.abs(p.x) <= COURT.HALF_W - 0.05) { // still inside: walk to the door first
    moveTo(inp, p, Math.abs(p.z - doorZ) > 0.5 ? s * 4.2 : s * 5.6, doorZ, skill, true);
    return;
  }
  moveTo(inp, p, b.x - s * 0.5, b.z - os * 0.3, skill, true);
  const d = Math.hypot(b.x - p.x, b.z - p.z);
  if (!p.swing && p.cd <= 0 && d < 1.3 && b.y > 0.1 && b.y < 2.2 && mem.react <= 0) inp.swing = true;
}

/** Press so the ball reaches the sweet spot about 0.17 s into the swing (where the racket face is square). */
function swingNow(b, dx, dz, dist, mem) {
  const closing = -(dx * b.vx + dz * b.vz) / (dist || 1);
  const ttc = Math.max(0, dist - 0.95) / Math.max(closing, 1.5);
  return dist < 1.25 || ttc <= 0.17 + (mem.jit || 0);
}

/** Serve: stronger levels wait for the ball at waist height, aim for the corners and add spin. */
function serve(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem, dt) {
  const rnd = game.rng;
  mem.serveT += dt;
  if (mem.serveWaitExtra === undefined) mem.serveWaitExtra = rnd() * 0.9;
  if (mem.serveT < 2.0 + skill.react * 2 + mem.serveWaitExtra || game.serveWait > 0) return; // give the receivers time to get set: no rushed serves
  const y = game.ball.y;
  // the racket meets the ball about 0.05 s after the press: judge the height then. Better levels wait for a tighter window.
  const yHit = serveBallHeight(game.time + 0.05);
  const win = skill.serveWindow ?? 0.5;
  if (Math.abs(yHit - 0.62) > win && mem.serveT < 4) return;
  void y;
  const boxSign = -game.rally.serveXSign;
  const edge = game.singles ? 3.2 : 4.4;
  // risk = how close to the lines the bot aims; the box is [0.3 .. edge] wide and 6.9 m deep
  const lateral = 1.4 + (edge - 2.6) * skill.serveRisk * rnd() + rnd() * 0.6; // bots no longer pick the extreme corners every time
  inp.ax = boxSign * Math.min(edge - 0.9, lateral);
  inp.az = os * (3.9 + 1.4 * skill.serveRisk * rnd());
  inp.mod = rnd() < skill.serveSpin ? (rnd() < 0.5 ? 3 : 1) : 0;
  inp.pace = (skill.servePace ?? 0.9) - rnd() * 0.06; // bots do not serve at full pace every time
  inp.swing = true;
  mem.serveT = 0;
  mem.serveWaitExtra = undefined;
}

/**
 * Doubles tactics, modelled on how real pairs move. A pair is almost always on the same line: both defending from the
 * baseline ('D') or both attacking at the net ('A'); the one-up-one-back split only exists briefly (the serve, the return,
 * a transition). The mode is decided ONCE per tick per team from shared information and stored on the game, so both
 * team-mates always agree without talking:
 *   incoming ball:  a lob or a deep ball → retreat (D); a short ball → go up (A)
 *   our own shot:   a good (deep, high) lob or a short low ball → go up (A); a poor lob or a drive → stay back (D)
 * A mode is held for a moment so the pair never flickers.
 */
function flightInfo(game) {
  const c = game._flight;
  if (c && c.tick === game.tickNo) return c;
  if (c && game.tickNo - c.tick < 4) return c; // recomputed every few ticks: it is only a tactical read
  const samples = predictBall(game.ball, 2.6);
  let apex = game.ball.y, firstFloor = null, netY = null, prevZ = game.ball.z;
  for (const s of samples) {
    apex = Math.max(apex, s.y);
    if (netY === null && (prevZ < 0) !== (s.z < 0)) netY = s.y;
    prevZ = s.z;
    if (!firstFloor && s.floor) firstFloor = s;
  }
  game._flight = { tick: game.tickNo, apex, firstFloor, netY };
  return game._flight;
}

function teamMode(game, p, skill) {
  const T = game.aiTeam || (game.aiTeam = [{ mode: 'D', since: 0, tick: -1 }, { mode: 'D', since: 0, tick: -1 }]);
  const S = T[p.team];
  if (S.tick === game.tickNo) return S.mode;
  S.tick = game.tickNo;
  const r = game.rally, b = game.ball;
  const f = flightInfo(game);
  const dFloor = f.firstFloor ? Math.abs(f.firstFloor.z) : 6; // distance from the net of the first bounce
  const lobby = f.apex > 3.2 && (f.netY === null || f.netY > 2.4);
  let want = null;
  const mate = game.players[p.slot ^ 1];
  if (mate.human) {
    // playing with a person: follow THEIR line (up when they are up, back when they are back) so the pair stays together; a
    // lob coming over still sends us back to cover it
    const incoming = r.lastTeam !== p.team;
    const lobIn = incoming && f.apex > 3.2 && (f.netY === null || f.netY > 2.4);
    if (lobIn) want = 'D';
    else if (Math.abs(mate.z) < 4.4) want = 'A';
    else if (Math.abs(mate.z) > 5.8) want = 'D';
    if (want && want !== S.mode && game.time - S.since >= 0.6) { S.mode = want; S.since = game.time; }
    return S.mode;
  }
  if (r.lastTeam !== p.team) { // the ball is coming to us
    const onMySide = p.team === 0 ? b.z < 0.5 : b.z > -0.5;
    if (lobby || dFloor > 6.2) want = 'D';
    else if (dFloor < 4.3 && b.y < 2.4 && skill.aggr >= 0.1) want = 'A';
    else if (!onMySide) want = null;
  } else { // we hit it
    if (lobby) want = dFloor > 7.2 ? 'A' : 'D'; // a deep lob wins the net, a poor one gets smashed
    else if (dFloor < 3.6 && f.apex < 2.0) want = 'A'; // a short low ball: they have to lift it
    else if (skill.aggr >= 0.1 && f.apex < 2.2 && game.players.filter((o) => o.team !== p.team && o.active).every((o) => Math.abs(o.z) > 6)) want = 'A';
  }
  if (want && want !== S.mode && game.time - S.since >= 0.8) { S.mode = want; S.since = game.time; }
  return S.mode;
}

function formation(inp, game, p, os, skill, mem) {
  const b = game.ball;
  if (game.singles) { moveTo(inp, p, clamp(b.x * 0.45, -2.4, 2.4), -os * ((skill.backZ ?? 7.6) - 0.1), skill, false); return; }
  const partner = game.players[p.slot ^ 1];
  const dp = Math.abs(p.z), dq = Math.abs(partner.z);
  const r = game.rally;
  if (r.serving || r.hits <= 1) {
    // the serve and the return keep the starting roles: server and receiver at the baseline, their partners at the net
    let net = dp < dq || (dp === dq && p.slot % 2 === 0);
    if (mem?.role) net = mem.role === 'net';
    const tz = -os * (net ? (skill.netZ ?? 3.4) : (skill.backZ ?? 7.6));
    const off = net ? (p.team === 0 ? -1.3 : 1.3) : (p.team === 0 ? 1.3 : -1.3);
    moveTo(inp, p, clamp(b.x * 0.45 + off, -3.6, 3.6), tz, skill, false);
    return;
  }
  // From the first return on, the pair moves as a unit: same line, side by side, shifting with the ball
  const mode = teamMode(game, p, skill);
  const lower = p.x < partner.x || (p.x === partner.x && p.slot < partner.slot); // each keeps their own side
  const att = mode === 'A';
  const centre = clamp(b.x * (att ? 0.5 : 0.55), -1.7, 1.7);
  const half = att ? 1.2 : 1.55; // the net pair is tighter; defenders cover more ground
  const tx = clamp(centre + (lower ? -half : half), -3.9, 3.9);
  const tz = -os * (att ? (skill.netZ ?? 3.4) - 0.5 : (skill.backZ ?? 7.6) - 0.4);
  if (mem) mem.role = Math.abs(p.z) < Math.abs(partner.z) ? 'net' : 'back';
  moveTo(inp, p, tx, tz, skill, false);
}

/** Pick the target and the shot: find the open space, then choose between lob, drop, smash, slice, drive. */
function chooseShot(inp, game, p, os, skill) {
  const rnd = game.rng;
  const b = game.ball;
  const opp = game.players.filter((o) => o.team !== p.team && o.active);
  const netOpp = opp.filter((o) => Math.abs(o.z) < 4.5).length;
  const deepOpp = opp.filter((o) => Math.abs(o.z) > 6.5).length;
  // Where to send it: weigh the open space (away from the opponents, the net player most of all) against changing the
  // direction of the ball (cross-court or down the line instead of always straight back) and keep away from the very
  // lines unless the bot is good enough to risk it.
  const lim = (game.singles ? 2.6 : 3.0) + 1.0 * skill.aggr;
  let bestX = 0, bestScore = -Infinity;
  const steps = game.singles ? [-2.6, -1.3, 0, 1.3, 2.6] : [-3.8, -2.5, -1.2, 0, 1.2, 2.5, 3.8];
  for (const x of steps) {
    if (Math.abs(x) > lim) continue;
    let space = Infinity;
    for (const o of opp) {
      const w = Math.abs(o.z) < 4.5 ? 1.35 : 1.0; // a player at the net covers less ground sideways but punishes a weak ball
      space = Math.min(space, Math.abs(o.x - x) * w + 0.12 * Math.abs(Math.abs(o.z) - 6));
    }
    const change = Math.abs(x - b.x) * 0.32; // send it somewhere else than where it came from
    const centre = Math.abs(x) < 0.7 ? -0.9 : 0; // the middle is the default of a weak player, not a plan
    const score = space + change + centre + rnd() * (1.6 - skill.aggr);
    if (score > bestScore) { bestScore = score; bestX = x; }
  }
  inp.ax = bestX + (rnd() - 0.5) * skill.noise;
  inp.az = os * (6 + rnd() * 2.6);
  inp.mod = 0;
  inp.lob = false;

  if (b.y > 2.1) { // overhead: remate when they are exposed, otherwise a placed bandeja or a víbora
    const r = rnd();
    if (r < skill.smash * (netOpp === 0 ? 1.0 : 0.6)) inp.mod = 0;
    else inp.mod = rnd() < 0.5 ? 2 : 1;
    if (inp.mod === 0) {
      inp.az = os * (netOpp ? 5 : 8);
      if (rnd() < skill.smash * 0.45 * (1 - skill.noise * 0.3)) inp.az = os * 3.4; // go for the kill: "por 4" (needs a clean contact)
    }
    return;
  }
  if (netOpp >= 1 && rnd() < skill.lob * 0.55) { // globo over the net players (not every time: it is a change of pace, not a habit)
    inp.lob = true; inp.az = os * 8.5;
  } else if (deepOpp === opp.length && rnd() < skill.aggr * 0.5) { // dejada against deep opponents
    inp.mod = 1; inp.az = os * 1.6;
  } else if (Math.abs(p.z) < 3.8 && rnd() < 0.25) { // chiquita at their feet
    inp.mod = 1; inp.az = os * 3;
  } else if (rnd() < skill.slice) { // slice to change the pace
    inp.mod = 3;
  }
}
===== END FILE: shared/ai.js =====

===== FILE: shared/career.js =====
// Career mode: a ladder of matches against named bots, from the club courts to the legends.
// Beat a stage to unlock the next one; earn 1-3 stars depending on how many games you concede.
//
// You always play on team 0 (slot 0). In doubles the partner is a bot of the stage's partner level.

export const CAREER = [
  { id: 1, court: 'indoor', looks: [{ arch: 2, hair: 6, skin: 1, racket: 3 }], tier: 'Club', name: 'First Steps', desc: 'A friendly 1v1 with the club coach.', mode: 'singles', difficulty: 'easy', opponents: ['Coach Pablo'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 2, xp: 60 },
  { id: 2, court: 'indoor', looks: [{ arch: 1, hair: 1, skin: 2, racket: 5 }, { arch: 1, hair: 4, skin: 0, racket: 1 }, { arch: 0, hair: 3, skin: 4, racket: 4 }], tier: 'Club', name: 'Club Doubles', desc: 'Your first doubles match, with Sofi at your side.', mode: 'doubles', difficulty: 'easy', partner: 'Sofi', partnerDiff: 'easy', opponents: ['Marta', 'Luis'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 3, xp: 80 },
  { id: 3, court: 'outdoor', looks: [{ arch: 0, hair: 3, skin: 4, racket: 1 }], tier: 'League', name: 'Sunday League', desc: 'Singles against the league regular.', mode: 'singles', difficulty: 'normal', opponents: ['Diego'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 4, xp: 110 },
  { id: 4, court: 'outdoor', looks: [{ arch: 3, hair: 2, skin: 3, racket: 0 }, { arch: 1, hair: 4, skin: 2, racket: 4 }, { arch: 2, hair: 5, skin: 1, racket: 2 }], tier: 'League', name: 'League Final', desc: 'Doubles final. Ana is your partner today.', mode: 'doubles', difficulty: 'normal', partner: 'Ana', partnerDiff: 'normal', opponents: ['Carla', 'Nico'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 6, xp: 150 },
  { id: 5, court: 'indoor', looks: [{ arch: 2, hair: 0, skin: 0, racket: 6 }], tier: 'Pro Circuit', name: 'Qualifier', desc: 'Singles qualifier against a seasoned pro.', mode: 'singles', difficulty: 'hard', opponents: ['Ruben'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 4, xp: 200 },
  { id: 6, court: 'night', looks: [{ arch: 3, hair: 1, skin: 1, racket: 7 }, { arch: 3, hair: 3, skin: 3, racket: 2 }, { arch: 0, hair: 6, skin: 4, racket: 6 }], tier: 'Pro Circuit', name: 'Pro Doubles', desc: 'Golden point doubles. Mateo plays with you.', mode: 'doubles', difficulty: 'hard', partner: 'Mateo', partnerDiff: 'hard', opponents: ['Elena', 'Javi'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 6, golden: true, xp: 260 },
  { id: 7, court: 'night', looks: [{ arch: 2, hair: 6, skin: 3, racket: 6 }], tier: 'Legends', name: 'The Wall', desc: 'Singles against the best defender in the circuit.', mode: 'singles', difficulty: 'expert', opponents: ['Maestro Vega'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 4, xp: 350 },
  { id: 8, court: 'night', looks: [{ arch: 3, hair: 4, skin: 3, racket: 7 }, { arch: 1, hair: 1, skin: 2, racket: 7 }, { arch: 2, hair: 6, skin: 3, racket: 6 }], tier: 'Legends', name: 'Grand Final', desc: 'Best of three, golden point, against the Legends.', mode: 'doubles', difficulty: 'expert', partner: 'Mateo', partnerDiff: 'hard', opponents: ['Maestra Rios', 'Maestro Vega'], sets: 3, gamesPerSet: 6, golden: true, xp: 500 },
];

/** Stars for a finished match from the human team's point of view. 0 = lost. */
export function starsFor(results) {
  if (results.winner !== 0) return 0;
  let mine = 0, theirs = 0;
  for (const [a, b] of results.score.sets) { mine += a; theirs += b; }
  const total = mine + theirs || 1;
  const share = theirs / total;
  return share <= 0.15 ? 3 : share <= 0.4 ? 2 : 1;
}

/** Ladder view from a {stageId: stars} map: stars per stage and which stages are unlocked. */
export function careerViewFrom(done = {}) {
  const stages = CAREER.map((s) => ({ ...s, stars: done[s.id] || 0, unlocked: s.id === 1 || (done[s.id - 1] || 0) >= 1 }));
  return { stages, stars: stages.reduce((a, s) => a + s.stars, 0), max: CAREER.length * 3 };
}

/** Apply a result to the stars map. Bonus XP is paid only for improving your best stars on a stage. */
export function applyCareerResult(done, stageId, stars) {
  const stage = CAREER.find((s) => s.id === stageId);
  const prev = done[stageId] || 0;
  const next = { ...done };
  let bonusXp = 0, unlocked = null;
  if (stage && stars > prev) {
    next[stageId] = stars;
    bonusXp = stage.xp * (stars - prev);
    if (prev === 0 && CAREER.some((s) => s.id === stageId + 1)) unlocked = stageId + 1;
  }
  return { done: next, stars, prev, bonusXp, unlocked };
}
===== END FILE: shared/career.js =====

// Career v2: seasons, tournaments with real brackets, ranking, objectives, training results, player development.
// Pure logic (no DOM, no storage handle of its own): it is unit-tested in Node and used by the browser.
// Matches the player does not play are SIMULATED with the same game engine and bots, so the brackets are real results.

import { ROSTER, ROSTER_BY_ID } from './roster.js';
import { PadelGame } from './game.js';
import { botInput, makeBotMemory } from './ai.js';

export const CAREER_VERSION = 2;
export const ATTRS = ['movement', 'power', 'stamina', 'groundstroke', 'volley', 'smash', 'lob', 'reaction', 'defense', 'variety'];
export const ATTR_LABELS = {
  movement: 'Speed (+1.5% running speed per level)', power: 'Power (+1.5% shot pace per level)', stamina: 'Stamina (tires 4% slower, recovers 4% faster)',
  groundstroke: 'Groundstroke consistency', volley: 'Volley control', smash: 'Smash power', lob: 'Lob precision',
  reaction: 'Reaction (longer hitting window)', defense: 'Defensive recovery', variety: 'Shot variety (more spin)',
};
export const ATTR_MAX = 10;

export const EVENTS = [

A small singles draw, a friendly crowd.', court: 'indoor', format: 'knockout', size: 4, mode: 'singles', pool: ['pablo', 'marta', 'luis', 'sofi'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 3, points: 30, coins: 60, xp: 80 },
  { id: 'club-doubles', season: 1, tier: 'Club', name: 'Club Doubles Cup', blurb: 'Your first doubles event. Sofi plays beside you.', court: 'indoor', format: 'knockout', size: 4, mode: 'doubles', partner: 'sofi', pool: ['marta', 'luis', 'pablo', 'diego', 'ana', 'carla'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 3, points: 40, coins: 80, xp: 100 },
  { id: 'sunday-league', season: 1, tier: 'League', name: 'Sunday League', blurb: 'Everyone plays everyone. Win your group to reach the final.', court: 'outdoor', format: 'group', size: 4, mode: 'singles', pool: ['diego', 'ana', 'carla', 'nico', 'marta'], sets: 1, gamesPerSet: 4, points: 60, coins: 100, xp: 140 },
  { id: 'league-final', season: 1, tier: 'League', name: 'League Final', blurb: 'The season finale under the Mediterranean sun.
